Every rank\n// generates its own rows of A directly (initialization is deterministic, so no\n// scatter is needed). B is needed in full by every rank; it is generated once\n// per node into an MPI shared-memory window, so there is no B broadcast and\n// only one copy per node. Both operands are generated directly in a packed\n// panel layout so the register-blocked micro-kernel streams contiguous data.\n// After the local multiply the row blocks of C are gathered on rank 0.\n//\n// Each C element is accumulated as a single fused multiply-add chain over k in\n// ascending order starting from 0, exactly like the original serial loop, so\n// results are bitwise identical.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nconstexpr size_t MR = 6;    // micro-tile rows (A panel width)\nconstexpr size_t NR = 8;    // micro-tile cols (B panel width)\nconstexpr size_t KC = 256;  // k-block (micro-panels stay in L1/L2)\nconstexpr size_t MC = 96;   // row block (A block stays in L2)\nconstexpr size_t NC = 1024; // column block (B block stays in L3)\n\ntypedef double v4d __attribute__((vector_size(32), aligned(8)));\n\nstatic inline v4d loadu(const double* p) {\n    v4d v;\n    std::memcpy(&v, p, sizeof(v));\n    return v;\n}\nstatic inline void storeu(double* p, const v4d& v) { std::memcpy(p, &v, sizeof(v)); }\n\n// C[0:6, 0:8] (row stride ldc) += Ap[k][0:6] * Bp[k][0:8] for k in [0, kc)\nstatic inline void microKernel(const size_t kc, const double* __restrict Ap,\n                               const double* __restrict Bp, double* __restrict C,\n                               const size_t ldc) {\n    v4d c00 = loadu(C + 0 * ldc), c01 = loadu(C + 0 * ldc + 4);\n    v4d c10 = loadu(C + 1 * ldc), c11 = loadu(C + 1 * ldc + 4);\n    v4d c20 = loadu(C + 2 * ldc), c21 = loadu(C + 2 * ldc + 4);\n    v4d c30 = loadu(C + 3 * ldc), c31 = loadu(C + 3 * ldc + 4);\n    v4d c40 = loadu(C + 4 * ldc), c41 = loadu(C + 4 * ldc + 4);\n    v4d c50 = loadu(C + 5 * ldc), c51 = loadu(C + 5 * ldc + 4);\n\n    for Ap[1]; c10 += a * b0; c11 += a * b1;\n        a = v4d{} + Ap[2]; c20 += a * b0; c21 += a * b1;\n        a = v4d{} + Ap[3]; c30 += a * b0; c31 += a * b1;\n        a = v4d{} + Ap[4]; c40 += a * b0; c41 storeu(C + 4 * ldc + 4, c41);\n    storeu(C + 5 * ldc, c50); storeu(C + 5 * ldc + 4, c51);\n}\n\n// Pack local rows [row0, row0 + rows) of A into panels of MR rows:\n// Ap[p * N * MR + k * MR + r] = A[row0 + p * MR + r][k] (zero padded)\nvoid initPackedA(std::vector<double>& Ap, const size_t N, const size_t row0, const size_t rows) {\n    const size_t panels = (rows + MR - 1) / MR;\n    Ap.assign(panels * N * MR, 0.0);\n    for (size_t p = 0; p < panels; ++p) {\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < MR; ++r) {\n            const size_t li = p * MR + r;\n            if (li >= rows) break;\n }\n}\n\n// Pack column panels [p0, p1) of B: Bp[p * N * NR + k * NR + c] = B[k][p * NR + c]\nvoid initPackedB(double* Bp, const size_t N, const size_t p0, const size_t p1) {\n    for (size_t p = p0; p < p1; ++p) {\n        double* panel = Bp + p * N * NR;\n        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {\n            for (size_t c = 0; c < NR; ++c) {\n                const size_t j = p * NR + c;\n                panel[k * NR + c] = (j < N) ? getPseudoRndValue(N, k, j) : 0.0;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Clocal (rows x N, row-major) = A_local * B using packed operands\nvoid matrixMultiplyLocal(const double* Ap, const double* Bp, double* C, const size_t N,\n                         const size_t rows) {\n    std::fill(C, C + rows * N, 0.0);\n    double tmp[MR * NR];\n\n true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Matrix size N (computes NxN * NxN) printUsage(argv[0]);\n            }\n            MPI_Finalize();\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    if (root) {\n        printf(\"Matrix Multiplication Benchmark\\n\");\n        printf(\"Matrix size: %zu x %zu\\n\", N, N);\n        printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n    }\n\n    // Row-block distribution of A and C\n    std::vector<int>   rowCounts[r] = static_cast<int>(hi - lo);\n    }\n    const size_t row0 = rowDispls[rank];\n    const size_t rows = rowCounts[rank];\n\n    // Node-local shared memory window holding packed B (one microKernel(const size_t kc, const double* __restrict Ap,\n                               const double* __restrict Bp, double* __restrict C,\n                               const size_t ldc) {\n Ap[1]; c10 += a * b0; c11 += a * b1;\n        a = v4d{} + Ap[2]; c20 += a * b0; c21 += a * b1;\n        a = v4d{} + Ap[3]; c30 += a * b0; c31 += a * b1;\n        a = v4d{} + Ap[4]; c40 += a * b0; c41 Ap.assign(panels * N * MR, 0.0);\n    for (size_t p = 0; p < panels; ++p) {\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < MR; ++r) {\n            const size_t li = p * MR + r;\n            if (li >= rows) break;\n            const size_t i = row0 + li;\n            double* dst = Ap.data() + p * N * MR + r;\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) dst[k * MR] = getPseudoRndValue(N, i, k);\n        }\n true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Matrix size N (computes NxN * NxN) (default: 512)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfeT5kw6s3cpnvGuq3Kd7","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The matmul benchmark now runs under MPI, and its output matches the serial version bit for bit. Only `matmul/matmul.cpp` and `matmul/CMakeLists.txt` were changed; the executable is still called `matmul` and is launched with `mpirun`.\n\n**How the work is split**\n- **2D grid of ranks:** `MPI_Dims_create` arranges the ranks in a grid, and each rank computes one block of C. This means each rank reads far less of A and B from memory than splitting by rows alone. With rows only, 128 ranks plateaued around 1.3 TFLOPS; with the grid they reach about 2.4.\n- **No sending of inputs:** the original fills A and B with a fixed formula, so each rank builds only the parts of A and B it needs. Nothing is copied between ranks before the multiply.\n- **Fast local multiply:** each rank uses a cache-blocked kernel that works on a 6×8 tile of C at a time, written with GCC vector extensions. The block sizes (`KC=512`, `MC=24`, `NC=256`) were tuned for 128 ranks.\n- **Collecting C:** rank 0 posts its receives before computing, so each rank's block lands straight in its place in the full C. The timed region covers the multiply plus this collection, so the reported time is when the full C is ready on rank 0.\n- Validation, `-r`/`-v` output and printing happen on rank 0 only. The exit code is shared with all ranks, so a failed validation still returns 1.\n\n**One thing to know about the numbers**\nSums are done in the same k order as the original, but every multiply and add is rounded separately (`-ffp-contract=off` in CMake). That is exactly what the source code says. The original binary is not quite consistent here: GCC computes most terms with a separate multiply and add, but the last term with a fused multiply-add when N is odd.\n- For even N, the result hash is identical to the original binary.\n- For odd N, the hash is identical to the original compiled with `-ffp-contract=off`. Against the default build it differs only in the last bit, well within the `-v` tolerance.\n\nI checked this for N = 1, 2, 7, 8, 100, 512, 999, 1000 and 1537 with 1–64 ranks, including uneven splits and runs with more ranks than rows. All matched and `-v` passed.\n\n**Performance** (2× EPYC 7763; the machine had other jobs running, load average ~54, so figures vary between runs)\n\n| Run | Time | GFLOPS |\n|---|---|---|\n| Original serial, N=2048 | 99 s | 0.17 |\n| 1 rank, N=4096 | 6.1 s | 22.5 |\n| 64 ranks, N=4096 | 164 ms | 838 |\n| 128 ranks, N=4096 | 132 ms | 1041 |\n| 128 ranks, N=8192 | — | about 2330–2530 |\n\nAt N=4096 with 128 ranks, a noticeable part of the time is collecting the 128 MB C matrix onto rank 0. The original requires the full C in one place, so I kept that.\n\nI tested on a single machine only, not across several nodes.\n\nTo build: `cmake -B build && cmake --build build`.
